Every Dog has Its Day: How Top‐Dog Versus Underdog Brand Positioning Affects Unethical Consumer Behavior
2026-05-27
Huijian Fu, Wei Cai, Linfeng Hu
EN

This study investigates how brand positioning (top-dog vs. underdog) influences unethical consumer behavior. Through six experiments with 1,398 participants, the researchers found that underdog brands reduce unethical consumer behavior by increasing consumer empathy toward the brand, which heightens anticipated guilt about violating moral norms. However, this effect is weakened when consumers have low moral identity or when the harm caused by unethical behavior is highly salient. The findings contribute to consumer ethics literature and provide practical guidance for brands to incorporate underdog narratives to curb behaviors such as cheating and coupon abuse.

JA

本研究は、ブランドポジショニング（トップドッグ型 vs. アンダードッグ型）が消費者の非倫理的行動にどのような影響を与えるかを検討した。1,398名を対象とした6つの実験を通じて、アンダードッグブランドは消費者のブランドへの共感を高め、道徳規範違反に対する予期的罪悪感を増幅させることで、非倫理的行動を抑制することが示された。ただし、この効果は消費者の道徳的アイデンティティが低い場合や、非倫理的行動による被害の顕著性が高い場合には弱まる。本研究は消費者倫理研究に貢献し、不正行為やクーポン悪用を抑制するためにアンダードッグ・ナラティブを活用する実務的指針を提供している。

Human in the Loop, or Perceived Oversight? The Psychological Inference That Drives AI Credibility
2026-05-25
Guneet Kaur Nagpal, June Cotte
AI
EN

This research investigates the psychological mechanism through which human-in-the-loop (HITL) AI systems build credibility, challenging the assumption that human oversight inherently increases trust. Through two experiments using AI-generated investment research, the authors found that human-supervised AI actually produced the lowest credibility among all conditions, and that 'perceived oversight adequacy'—the inference that competent review occurred—is the dominant mediator of the source-to-credibility relationship. The key contribution is reframing the HITL debate from whether to add human oversight to how to effectively signal that the analytical process was sound.

JA

本研究は、人間参加型（HITL）AIシステムが信頼性を構築する心理的メカニズムを解明し、人間の監視が常に信頼を高めるという前提に疑問を呈している。AI生成の投資リサーチを用いた2つの実験を通じて、人間監視付きAIが全条件中最も低い信頼性を示すこと、そして「知覚された監視の適切性」（有能な人物がレビューしたという推論）が情報源と信頼性の関係を媒介する主要因であることを発見した。本研究の貢献は、HITLの議論を「人間を加えるべきか」から「分析プロセスが適切であったことをいかにシグナルするか」へと再構成した点にある。

The Silent Guardian: Symmetry's Subtle Influence on Safety Cues and Consumer Behavior
2026-05-23
Yi Huang, Hanwen Bi, Wei Zhao, Fenghua Wang, Yu Pan
AI
EN

This study investigates how visual symmetry in product presentations and brand imagery influences consumer behavior through perceived safety. Using a mixed-methods approach combining Google Trends analysis, archival data, and five laboratory experiments, the researchers demonstrate that symmetrical elements increase perceived safety, which in turn enhances consumer responses across various product categories. The effect is particularly strong for products perceived as unsafe, and both vertical and horizontal symmetry produce comparable effects. This research contributes to marketing literature by identifying symmetry as a non-semantic structural cue that can be strategically used to shape safety perceptions and influence consumer behavior.

JA

本研究は、製品プレゼンテーションやブランドイメージにおける視覚的対称性が、知覚された安全性を通じて消費者行動にどのように影響するかを検討した。Googleトレンド分析、アーカイブデータ、5つの実験室実験を組み合わせた混合研究法を用い、対称的な要素が知覚された安全性を高め、それが様々な製品カテゴリーにおける消費者反応を促進することを実証した。この効果は安全でないと知覚される製品で特に顕著であり、垂直対称性と水平対称性は同等の効果を示した。本研究は、対称性が安全性の知覚を形成し消費者行動に影響を与える非意味的な構造的手がかりとして戦略的に活用できることを明らかにし、マーケティング研究に貢献している。

The Embodiment of Vulnerability: How Consumers Judge Empathy Gaps in Artificial Intelligence‐Driven Healthcare
2026-05-20
Mariana Girão Carrilho, Diego Costa Pinto, Saleh Shuqair, Márcia Maurer Herter, Anna S. Mattila
AIartificial intelligence
EN

This research investigates how consumers perceive empathy from AI versus human healthcare providers depending on the type of vulnerability experienced. Drawing on Embodiment Theory and Mind-Body Dualism, the authors conducted two controlled experiments and text mining of health app reviews to test their framework. The key finding reveals a vulnerability-contingent dilemma: humans are perceived as more empathetic for embodied vulnerability (physical pain), while AI achieves comparable empathy perceptions for disembodied vulnerability (mental health contexts). This contributes to the literature by introducing the concept of 'embodiment of vulnerability' and demonstrating how it systematically shapes consumer judgments of empathic care capacity in AI-driven healthcare.

JA

本研究は、消費者が経験する脆弱性のタイプに応じて、AIと人間の医療提供者に対する共感認知がどのように異なるかを検討している。身体化理論と心身二元論に基づき、2つの統制実験とヘルスアプリレビューのテキストマイニングを実施した。主要な発見として、脆弱性に依存したジレンマが明らかになった：身体的脆弱性（身体的痛み）では人間がより共感的と認知される一方、非身体的脆弱性（メンタルヘルス）ではAIが人間と同等の共感認知を達成した。本研究は「脆弱性の身体化」という概念を導入し、それがAI医療における共感的ケア能力の消費者判断を体系的に形成することを示した点で貢献している。

How Divisible Donation Amounts Increase Charitable Giving
2026-05-20
Xiaohong Zhao, Zhiyong Yang, Shige Zhao
AI
EN

This research investigates how the divisibility of suggested donation amounts affects charitable giving behavior. Through seven studies combining field and experimental methods, the authors demonstrate that divisible amounts (e.g., $20) increase donations compared to non-divisible amounts (e.g., $19), mediated by donors' perceived breadth of impact and fairness. The effect is moderated by need type, being attenuated for survival needs but amplified for developmental needs. These findings contribute to the charitable giving literature by identifying a novel numerical factor influencing donation decisions and offer practical guidance for nonprofit organizations in designing effective fundraising appeals.

JA

本研究は、提示される寄付金額の割り切れやすさ（可分性）が寄付行動にどのように影響するかを検討している。7つの研究を通じて、割り切れる金額（例：20ドル）は割り切れない金額（例：19ドル）と比較して寄付を増加させることが示され、この効果は寄付者が認知する影響の広がりと公平性によって媒介されることが明らかになった。さらに、この効果はニーズの種類によって調整され、生存ニーズでは減衰し、発達ニーズでは増幅される。本研究は、寄付意思決定に影響を与える新たな数値的要因を特定し、非営利組織の効果的な募金活動の設計に実践的示唆を提供する点で貢献している。

When Social Media Ideals Backfire: How Idealized Digital Standards Undermine Self‐Efficacy and Goal Attainability
2026-05-19
Rafael Wagner, Diego Costa Pinto, Saleh Shuqair, Ana Valenzuela, Barry J. Babin
AI
EN

This research examines how exposure to idealized standards on social media affects consumers' motivation during long-term goal pursuit. Using five studies including text-mining analysis and controlled experiments, the authors find that idealized digital representations undermine motivation by inflating perceived goal distance and eroding self-efficacy, rather than by increasing perceived effort. The negative effects are amplified by performance-oriented mindsets but attenuated by mastery-oriented mindsets. The study contributes to goal systems and self-regulation theories by identifying idealized standards as external goal-regulating systems and proposing a content-design intervention that emphasizes means over end states to preserve motivation.

JA

本研究は、ソーシャルメディア上の理想化された基準への接触が、長期的な目標追求における消費者のモチベーションにどのような影響を与えるかを検討している。テキストマイニング分析と統制実験を含む5つの研究を通じて、理想化されたデジタル表現は努力の認知を高めるのではなく、目標との距離感を膨張させ自己効力感を低下させることでモチベーションを損なうことを明らかにした。この負の効果はパフォーマンス志向のマインドセットで増幅され、習熟志向のマインドセットで緩和される。本研究は、理想化された基準を外的な目標調整システムとして位置づけ、理想的な最終状態ではなく達成手段を強調するコンテンツデザイン介入を提案することで、目標システム理論および自己制御理論に貢献している。

Liking Without Endorsing: Consumer Dilemmas in Responses to AI‐Generated Music
2026-05-12
Andrew Edelblum, Joshua Poe
AI
EN

This study investigates why AI-generated music achieves market success despite documented consumer aversion to AI creativity. Through three experiments, a pilot study, and supplemental tests, the authors demonstrate that while consumers initially expect lower enjoyment from AI-generated music, these differences diminish after direct listening exposure—a pattern not observed in cognitively mediated domains like writing. However, this aesthetic convergence does not indicate full acceptance; consumers experience evaluative conflict and remain reluctant to engage deeply through behaviors like relistening and sharing. The research contributes a novel framework explaining how affective processing attenuates but does not fully resolve AI authorship concerns in music consumption.

JA

本研究は、AI生成コンテンツへの消費者の忌避が報告されているにもかかわらず、AI生成音楽が市場で成功を収めている理由を検討した。3つの実験、パイロット研究、および補足テストを通じて、消費者はAI生成音楽に対して当初は低い期待を示すものの、実際に聴取した後にはその差異が縮小することを明らかにした。ただし、この審美的評価の収束は完全な受容を意味せず、消費者は好意的な聴取体験とAI創造性への嫌悪的信念の間で評価的葛藤を経験し、再聴取や共有といった深い関与行動には依然として消極的であった。本研究は、感情的処理がAI著作権への懸念を緩和するものの完全には解消しないことを示し、AI生成創造物の消費者評価に関する新たな理論的枠組みを提供している。

AI‐Driven Personalization in Social Media Advertising: How Tracking Awareness and eWOM Homophily Shape Trust and Consumer Responses
2026-05-11
Andrea Heintz Tangari, My (Myla) Bui, Anjala S. Krishen, Elyria Kemp
AI
EN

This research investigates how consumers' awareness of digital tracking for personalized social media advertising affects trust, purchase intentions, and willingness to pay. Through three experiments, the authors found that higher social media usage and perceived influence from digital advertising increase trust in ad information, which subsequently enhances purchase intentions and WTP. The study also reveals that electronic word-of-mouth (eWOM) homophily effects are moderated by personalization levels—consumers value similarity cues more under less-personalized conditions but rely more on tracking-based cues when ads are highly personalized. The research contributes to trust transfer theory by demonstrating how tracking awareness and social similarity cues interact to shape consumer responses in social media advertising contexts.

JA

本研究は、ソーシャルメディア広告のパーソナライゼーションに用いられるデジタルトラッキングに対する消費者の認知が、信頼、購買意図、支払意思額（WTP）にどのように影響するかを検証した。3つの実験を通じて、ソーシャルメディアの利用頻度が高く、デジタル広告からの影響を強く認識している消費者ほど広告情報への信頼が高まり、それが購買意図とWTPを向上させることが明らかになった。また、電子口コミ（eWOM）の類似性効果はパーソナライゼーションの程度によって調整され、低パーソナライズ条件では類似した他者からの情報を重視するが、高度にパーソナライズされた条件ではトラッキングに基づく手がかりにより依存することが示された。本研究は、トラッキング認知と社会的類似性の手がかりがソーシャルメディア広告環境における消費者の信頼と反応を形成する相互作用メカニズムを解明し、信頼転移理論に貢献している。

The AI Agent in the Room: Rethinking Consumer Decision‐Making in the Age of Autonomous AI
2026-05-11
Anil Bilgihan, Massimiliano Ostinelli, Melanie Lorenz, Ye Zhang
AI
EN

This editorial addresses the lack of a shared definition and theoretical framework for AI agents (AIAs) in marketing scholarship. The authors propose a definition of AIAs based on three features (autonomy, adaptive learning/memory, and tool-enabled connectivity), introduce a typology distinguishing advisory from transactional AIAs, and present a three-stage framework of consumer decision-making with AIAs (prompting, processing, and response) incorporating relational capital and AI learning feedback loops. Through systematic mapping of AIA-focused articles in leading marketing journals (2020-2026), they reveal that existing research heavily focuses on consumer responses while neglecting prompting behavior, agent cognition, privacy, transparency, and relational dynamics. The paper argues that transactional AIAs represent a paradigm shift where consumers are no longer sole cognitive actors and preferences are co-constructed through human-agent conversation, outlining a research agenda to address these gaps.

JA

本論説は、マーケティング研究においてAIエージェント（AIA）の共通定義と理論的枠組みが欠如している問題に取り組んでいる。著者らは、自律性・適応学習と記憶・ツール連携という3つの特徴に基づくAIAの定義を提案し、助言型と取引型AIAを区別する類型論を導入するとともに、消費者のプロンプティング、AIAの処理、消費者の反応という3段階の意思決定フレームワークを提示した。2020年から2026年の主要マーケティングジャーナルの体系的マッピングにより、既存研究がAI推奨への消費者反応に偏重し、プロンプティング行動、エージェント認知、プライバシー、透明性、関係性ダイナミクスを軽視していることを明らかにした。取引型AIAの台頭により、消費者がもはや購買プロセスにおける唯一の認知主体ではなく、選好が人間とエージェントの会話を通じて共同構築されるというパラダイムシフトを主張し、研究課題を提示している。

Externalizing the Mental Struggle: How Anthropomorphism Enhances Health Engagement via Emotional Detachment and Self‐Efficacy
2026-05-11
Jiancai Liao, Shoujiang Zhou, Khloé Qi Kang
AI
EN

This research investigates how anthropomorphism can enhance engagement with mental health communications, addressing the unique challenge that mental disorders create a sense of self-illness fusion unlike physical illnesses. Through four studies (N = 1328), the authors demonstrate that anthropomorphizing mental disorders as independent, human-like entities promotes protective behaviors through a coping appraisal pathway: emotional detachment leads to enhanced self-efficacy, which in turn increases health engagement. The effect is specific to independent (not dependent) anthropomorphism and is attenuated among individuals who hold natural kind beliefs viewing mental disorders as biologically fixed. This research contributes to marketing theory by identifying a novel mechanism distinct from threat appraisal pathways previously established for physical diseases.

JA

本研究は、精神障害が身体疾患とは異なり自己と病気の融合感を生み出すという固有の課題に対し、擬人化がメンタルヘルスコミュニケーションへの関与をいかに高めるかを検討した。4つの研究（N=1328）を通じて、精神障害を独立した人間のような存在として擬人化することで、感情的な切り離し（emotional detachment）が生じ、自己効力感が向上し、保護行動が促進されるという対処評価経路を介した連続媒介効果を実証した。この効果は独立型の擬人化にのみ見られ、精神障害を生物学的に固定されたものと捉える本質主義的信念を持つ個人では効果が減弱することが示された。本研究は、身体疾患で確立された脅威評価経路とは異なる擬人化の新たなメカニズムを特定し、マーケティング理論に貢献している。

Bolstering Heroism in Crisis Communications: How Communicating Employees' Performance can Improve Consumers' Responses
2026-05-06
I. Baghi, Ilaria Baghi
AI
EN

This study investigates whether attributing heroism to employees in crisis communications can enhance consumer responses following external crises such as weather disruptions or cyberattacks. Using five experiments, the research develops a moderated mediation model showing that heroism-framed recovery messages improve positive word of mouth, purchase intentions, and donation behavior, with these effects mediated by admiration toward employees. The impact is moderated by boundary conditions: heroism appeals are more effective when risk is salient but weakened when high job demands or strong employer reputation normalize exceptional performance. This research contributes to marketing literature by demonstrating the strategic value of heroism attributions in crisis communications.

JA

本研究は、気象災害やサイバー攻撃などの外部危機後のクライシスコミュニケーションにおいて、従業員にヒロイズム（英雄性）を帰属させることが消費者反応を向上させるかを検討した。5つの実験を通じて、ヒロイズムを強調した回復メッセージが口コミ、購買意図、寄付行動を改善し、この効果が従業員への称賛感情によって媒介されることを示す調整媒介モデルを構築した。ヒロイズムの効果はリスクが顕著な場合に強まる一方、高い職務要求や雇用主の高評判といった文脈的手がかりが優れたパフォーマンスを常態化させる場合には弱まることが明らかになった。本研究は、クライシスコミュニケーションにおけるヒロイズム帰属の戦略的価値を実証し、マーケティング研究に貢献している。

Visual Implied Motion in Marketing: A Dual‐Route Framework of Perceptual Persuasion
2026-05-06
Tianyi Zhang, Charles Spence
AI
EN

This systematic review examines how implied motion (depicted movement in static images) influences consumer persuasion in marketing contexts. By synthesizing 31 experimental studies within the Elaboration Likelihood Model framework, the authors identify a dual-route mechanism: implied motion enhances perceptual salience and attention, then operates either through central-route elaboration (via mental simulation and narrative transportation) or peripheral-route processing (triggering heuristic inferences like energy and freshness). The effects are moderated by consumer-level factors (consumption goals, involvement) and design-level factors (product type, brand-motion fit). This review provides the first theoretically integrated framework explaining how dynamic cues in static visuals shape consumer attitudes, purchase intentions, and willingness to pay.

JA

本論文は、パッケージ、ロゴ、広告などの静止画像における「暗示的動き（implied motion）」が消費者の説得にどのように影響するかを体系的にレビューしたものである。31件の実験研究を精緻化見込みモデル（ELM）の枠組みで統合し、暗示的動きが知覚的顕著性と注意を高めた後、中心ルート（メンタルシミュレーションや物語への没入を通じた精緻化）または周辺ルート（エネルギーや新鮮さなどのヒューリスティック推論）という二重経路で作用することを明らかにした。これらの効果は消費者要因（消費目的、関与度）とデザイン要因（製品タイプ、ブランドと動きの適合性）により調整される。本レビューは、静止画像内の動的手がかりが消費者の態度や購買意図を形成するメカニズムについて、初めて理論的に統合された枠組みを提供している。

Narrative Positioning of a Fake Human: The Credibility of Virtual Influencers in Their Communication Styles
2026-05-05
Karina Sokolova, Charles Pérez, Ekaterina Besson
AI
EN

This research investigates how communication styles, specifically pronoun usage, affect the credibility and persuasiveness of virtual influencers compared to human influencers. Study 1 analyzed 391 virtual influencers on X, identifying four pronoun-based communication styles (agent-centered, group-centered, audience-centered, and informational), while Study 2 experimentally examined how self- versus audience-centered messaging affects audience perceptions in fitness Instagram posts. The findings reveal that audience-centered messages enhance perceptions of anthropomorphism for virtual influencers, which in turn increases credibility and self-efficacy, ultimately predicting exercise intentions. This research contributes to influencer marketing literature by demonstrating that pronoun-based communication style indirectly affects persuasion outcomes through anthropomorphism, suggesting virtual influencers' potential for both marketing and health promotion.

Emotional Showdown: Can Virtual Endorsers Out‐Express Human Endorsers in Digital Advertising?
2026-05-05
Chenying Hai, Jun Yan, Yinghao He, Yinglu Wu
AI
EN

This study investigates whether virtual endorsers can convey positive emotions as effectively as human endorsers in digital advertising. Using field data analysis with a large language model and five controlled experiments, the research found that human endorsers outperform virtual endorsers in conveying abstract emotions (e.g., hope, gratitude), while no significant difference exists for concrete emotions (e.g., happiness, excitement). Emotional contagion and perceived expectation violation serve as parallel mediators, and perceived self-endorser attitude similarity can mitigate virtual endorsers' disadvantage with abstract emotions. These findings contribute to understanding emotional dynamics in endorser marketing and offer practical guidance for selecting endorsers based on emotion type.

JA

本研究は、デジタル広告においてバーチャルインフルエンサーが人間のインフルエンサーと同等にポジティブな感情を伝達できるかを検証した。大規模言語モデルを用いたフィールドデータ分析と5つの統制実験の結果、希望や感謝などの抽象的感情の伝達では人間が優位である一方、幸福や興奮などの具体的感情では両者に有意差がないことが明らかになった。感情伝染と期待違反の知覚が並行的な媒介要因として機能し、自己とインフルエンサーの態度類似性の知覚がバーチャルインフルエンサーの抽象的感情伝達における不利を緩和することも示された。本研究は感情マーケティングにおける両者の感情ダイナミクスの理解を深め、感情タイプに基づくインフルエンサー選定への実務的示唆を提供する。

When and How Does Generative AI Outperform Human Agents in Service Recovery?
2026-05-05
Hesam Olya, Amin Nazifi, Babak Taheri, Huaiyu Li
AIartificial intelligence
EN

This study investigates when and how generative AI (GenAI) outperforms human agents in service failure and recovery contexts. Using qualitative interviews, a lab experiment, and four online experiments in live streaming settings, the research applies Cognitive Appraisal Theory to compare GenAI and human agents across single and double deviation scenarios. Key findings reveal that GenAI leads to higher repurchase intention after single failures, while human agents perform better after double deviations; however, high empathy equalizes outcomes and compensation enhances GenAI's effectiveness beyond human agents, with promotion emotion mediating these effects. The study contributes actionable guidance for practitioners on strategically deploying GenAI versus human agents based on failure complexity.

JA

本研究は、サービス失敗と回復（SFR）の文脈において、生成AI（GenAI）が人間のエージェントをいつ、どのように上回るかを検討している。質的インタビュー、ラボ実験、ライブストリーミング環境での4つのオンライン実験を用い、認知的評価理論（CAT）に基づいて単一失敗と二重逸脱シナリオにおけるGenAIと人間エージェントの効果を比較した。主要な発見として、単一失敗後はGenAIがより高い再購買意図をもたらす一方、二重逸脱後は人間エージェントが優れるが、高い共感性はGenAIの成果を人間エージェントと同等にし、補償はGenAIの回復成果を人間エージェント以上に高めることが示され、これらの効果はプロモーション感情によって媒介される。本研究は、失敗の複雑さに応じてGenAIと人間エージェントを戦略的に使い分けるための実務的指針を提供している。

Inside the Quiet Luxury Mindset: How Do Self‐Related Orientations Influence Inconspicuous Luxury Consumption?
2026-05-04
Xiaoyu Zhang, Eugene Cheng-Xi Aw, Ian Phau
AI
EN

This study investigates how self-related orientations (self-monitoring, self-directed pleasure, and self-identity) influence inconspicuous luxury consumption. Using a mixed-methods approach combining PLS-SEM, fsQCA, and qualitative interviews with Chinese luxury consumers, the research found that association with elite and privacy are primary motivations for inconspicuous luxury consumption, with self-monitoring enhancing elite association, self-directed pleasure fostering privacy motivation, and self-identity reinforcing both. The qualitative findings further revealed that inconspicuous luxury consumption emerges from the interplay of social structure, psychological motivation, and behavioral strategies guided by cultural capital and self-oriented aesthetics. This study contributes to luxury consumption literature by revealing the mechanism through which self-related orientations drive inconspicuous consumption and offers practical implications for luxury brand marketing strategies.

Companionship as Service: The Psychological Efficacy of Commercialized Interactions in Reducing Loneliness
2026-05-02
Chen Wanyi, Ruizhi Yuan, Martin J. Liu
AI
EN

This study investigated whether paid companionship services can effectively reduce loneliness and through what psychological mechanisms they operate. Using a mixed-methods design combining qualitative exploration (Study 1) and two experiments (Study 2), the researchers found that paid companionship significantly reduced immediate loneliness compared to control conditions, with this effect statistically mediated by enhanced state social connection and moderated by social presence (specifically, out-group audience presence amplified the effect). The research also identified potential long-term challenges including emotional dependency and diminishing marginal utility. This study provides the first empirical evidence for the psychological efficacy of commercialized companionship, contributing a novel conceptualization of market-mediated relational exchange to service literature.

Eliciting Mental Time Travel Through Digital Product Displays and Its Positive Influence on Purchase‐Oriented Outcomes
2026-05-02
Jennifer Brannon Barhorst, Nina Krey, Graeme McLean, Heiner Evanschitzky
AI
EN

This study investigates how digital product displays elicit mental time travel (MTT) to the past and future, and how this cognitive process influences purchase-oriented outcomes including inspiration to purchase, booking intention, and willingness to pay. Using three experimental studies across different product categories (snack food, cruise travel, and wine), the authors demonstrate that past MTT positively influences future MTT, which in turn enhances all three purchase outcomes. The research also reveals that immersion does not moderate these relationships, while habitual fiction engagement (brain plasticity) strengthens the effect of future MTT on willingness to pay. This work contributes to marketing literature by introducing mental time travel as a novel mechanism explaining how digital product displays drive consumer purchase behavior.

Brand Consumption Denial: Conceptualization, Scale Development, and Validation
2026-05-02
Ulun Akturan, Nilşah Cavdar Aksoy, Deniz Kuter
AI
EN

This study introduces and conceptualizes 'brand consumption denial,' a novel construct describing consumers who avoid brands publicly while continuing to use them privately. Through five studies, the authors developed and validated a multidimensional scale measuring two dimensions: social judgment–driven and secret love–driven brand consumption denial. Key findings reveal these dimensions have divergent effects on consumer-brand relationships, with social judgment–driven denial weakening brand attachment and recommendation intention, while secret love–driven denial sustains attachment and reinforces recommendation intention without self-disclosure. This research contributes to brand relationship literature by addressing an understudied phenomenon and providing a reliable measurement tool for future research.

The Positive Impact of Product Orderliness on Intentions to Consume Vices
2026-04-27
Aekyoung Kim, Saerom Lee
EN

This research investigates how product display orderliness affects vice consumption. Through multiple studies, the authors demonstrate that orderly (vs. disorderly) product displays increase consumers' intentions to consume vices by enhancing their sense of control over consumption situations, which reduces their motivation to self-regulate indulgent behavior. The effect does not apply to virtue consumption due to its inherently high baseline sense of control, and can be mitigated by enhancing perceived control through other means such as episodic recall or providing choices. This research contributes to the literature on environmental influences, perceived control, and indulgent consumption while offering practical implications for retail display strategies.

A Sociocultural Examination of Religiosity's Role in Coupon Proneness
2026-04-27
William Ding, David E. Sprott, Jeff Joireman
EN

This study examines how religiosity, as a sociocultural factor, influences consumers' coupon proneness and redemption behavior. Using both secondary data from Google Trends and primary survey data, the researchers found that higher religiosity is associated with increased coupon use because religious consumers make more benevolent inferences about coupons, viewing them as legitimate cost-reduction tools offered by businesses. The study further demonstrates that frugality-based motivated reasoning, rather than generalized social trust, better explains this benevolent interpretation. These findings extend prior research beyond traditional demographic and marketing factors, offering marketers a new segmentation variable to address the prevalent challenge of low coupon redemption rates.

Partitioned or All‐Inclusive Pricing? It Depends on the Brand–Customer Relationship Type
2026-04-22
Hsin‐Hsien Liu, Hsuan‐Yi Chou, Xin‐Yi Chen
AI
EN

This study investigates how the effectiveness of partitioned pricing versus all-inclusive pricing differs depending on whether customers have communal or exchange relationships with brands. Through three experiments, the researchers found that consumers in exchange relationships exhibit lower purchase intentions for partitioned pricing compared to all-inclusive pricing due to more concrete information processing and lower brand trust, while this difference diminishes in communal relationships. This research contributes to pricing literature by demonstrating the moderating role of brand-customer relationship types and providing practical guidance for marketers to align pricing strategies with customer relationship contexts.

Dual Pathways of Loneliness in the Marketplace: Emotional Attachment, Empowerment, and Evaluations of Brand Warmth and Brand Competence
2026-04-22
Aulona Ulqinaku, Gülen Sarial‐Abi, Selma Kadić‐Maglajlić
AI
EN

This research investigates how different types of loneliness affect consumer preferences for warm versus competent brands. Using six studies including a national field study, incentive-aligned choice experiments, longitudinal brand-level panel data, and online experiments, the authors demonstrate that individual loneliness (personal social disconnection) activates agency-restoration motives, leading to preferences for competent brands, while collective loneliness (shared disconnection at the context level) triggers emotional attachment and preferences for warm brands. The key contribution is establishing that loneliness operates through dual pathways depending on the referent level, resolving prior inconsistencies in the literature. Managerially, firms can enhance message effectiveness by matching competence- or warmth-focused communications to observable social contexts.

AIartificial intelligence

This study investigates how consumers interpret and evaluate AI voice assistants in everyday interactions, focusing on perceived cognitive capability and anthropomorphism. Framing the Feed: How Visual Elements Shape Manipulative Intent Inferences and Consumer Responses to In‐Feed Ads on Social Media
2026-04-16
Patricia Rossi, Felipe Pantoja, Philippe Odou
AI
EN

This research investigates how visual elements in social media in-feed advertisements influence consumer resistance and brand responses. Using four studies including a field experiment, the authors found that lifestyle photos (showing products in use) generate more favorable brand attitudes, stronger purchase intentions, and higher click-through rates compared to product-only photos. Drawing on the Persuasion Knowledge Model, they demonstrate that this effect is mediated by reduced inferences of manipulative intent, and disappears under high cognitive load when consumers lack resources to process persuasive motives. The study contributes to understanding visual persuasion by identifying manipulative intent as a distinct psychological mechanism linking visual elements to advertising effectiveness.

AI
EN

Riding Solo: The Impact of Relationship Status on Brand Attachment
2026-04-10
Rui Chen, Kunyu Wu, Zhaoyan Shang
AI
EN

This research examines how being single affects consumers' brand attachment, proposing attachment avoidance as the mediating mechanism. Across five studies conducted in China and the United States with 2,015 participants, the authors found that single individuals exhibit weaker brand attachment than non-singles because singlehood elevates attachment avoidance, which subsequently reduces affiliative connections with brands. The study identifies boundary conditions including secure-attachment priming, materialism, and cultural context (individualistic vs. family-centered societies) that moderate this effect. Theoretically, this work contributes by demonstrating an assimilation process rather than compensatory attachment, extending attachment theory from interpersonal relationships to consumer-brand relationships.

God's Presence in the Aisle: How God Salience Encourages Preference for Ultra‐Processed Foods
2026-04-09
Ali Gohary, Hean Tat Keh
AI
EN

This research investigates how God-related cues influence consumer food choices, specifically preferences for ultra-processed foods. Through six studies including a field experiment, the authors find that high God salience increases consumers' preference for ultra-processed foods over unprocessed alternatives. This effect is mediated by perceived healing by God—consumers believe God will mitigate negative health consequences—and is moderated by the perceived predictability of God's intervention, with the effect emerging only when intervention is seen as predictable. The findings contribute to compensatory control theory and the literature on religion and consumer decision-making, offering implications for promoting healthier food choices.

Surprise Marketing
2026-04-09
Xin‐Jean Lim, Weng Marc Lim, Park Thaichon
AI
EN

This article addresses the fragmented scholarly understanding of surprise marketing by introducing the PIEL framework, which integrates four core tenets: Probabilistic outcomes, Intentional design, Exclusive ownership, and Low stake offerings. Through comprehensive analysis of contemporary marketing studies and marketplace practices, the authors examine the evolution of surprise marketing across three strategic phases (emergence, engagement, and immersion) in response to phygital trends and changing consumer expectations. The key finding reveals a duality in surprise marketing: while it evokes powerful positive emotions, it can paradoxically heighten consumer vulnerability, skepticism, and perceived unfairness. The article contributes by identifying critical research gaps and proposing future directions including social change applications, inclusivity, ethical concerns around dynamic pricing, consumer wellbeing, and paradoxical emotions.

2026-04-07

AI

On the Transformative Nature of Luxury Consumption and Consumer Well‐Being: A Systematic Literature Review and Research Agenda
2026-04-04
Solon Magrizos, Maria C. Voutsa, Minas N. Kastanakis
AI
EN

This systematic literature review examines how and why luxury consumption contributes to consumer well-being, synthesizing insights from 109 academic articles. The study reveals that traditional/masstige luxury primarily delivers temporal hedonic gratification, while unconventional luxury (experiential, ethical, sustainable) aligns more closely with eudaimonic well-being. The authors propose a dual-pathway conceptual framework reconceptualizing luxury as a driver of self-transformation through intrapersonal and sociomoral pathways, identifying key socio-psychological, cultural, and contextual mechanisms. The research contributes seven future-oriented propositions and provides practical design levers for managers to align luxury with sustainability and post-materialist values.

Real vs. Imagined Sensory Inputs in Virtual Reality: A Construal‐Level Based Conceptual Framework and Future Research Directions
2026-03-31
Seth Ketron, Kirsten Cowan, Uyen Thy Duong, Nikhil Jangir
AI
EN

This research examines how sensory inputs (audiovisual, olfactory, haptic, and gustatory) in virtual reality environments affect consumer experiences, specifically exploring the distinction between real and imagined sensory modalities. Using construal level theory, the authors develop a conceptual framework with propositions suggesting that the number and nature of sensory inputs influence proximity and cognitive load, which respectively enhance and detract from immersion. Countering Social Information: Low Identity Integration Leads to Nonconformity in Consumption Choices
2026-03-31
Kathrin J. Hanek, Stephen Garcia
AI
EN

This research examines how individual differences in managing multiple identities (identity integration) moderate the processing of social information in consumption choices. How Downsizing a Typical Product at a Stable Unit Price Recalibrates Price Perceptions: A Contrast–Assimilation Account of Perceived Category and New‐Product Expensiveness
2026-03-31
Frank Goedertier, Karen Gorissen, Bert Weijters
AI
EN

This research investigates how downsizing a typical product in an assortment while maintaining a stable unit price affects consumers' perceived expensiveness of the category and newly introduced products. Through three experimental studies (N=447, 476, 294) using visual scenario-based online surveys with FMCG products (chocolate and soft drinks), the authors demonstrate that this tactic reduces perceived expensiveness of the overall category assortment but simultaneously increases perceived expensiveness of new products within that assortment. These opposing effects are explained through contrast-assimilation psychological mechanisms, with assortment size identified as a moderator and unit price disclosure attenuating these effects. The study contributes to reference price research and provides retailers with a margin-preserving alternative to steep discounting for managing price perceptions.

2026-03-30
Vik Naidoo
AIartificial intelligence
EN

This paper investigates how consumers respond to carbon-transparency cues that reveal the environmental costs of AI-enabled services, addressing the ethical dilemma between digital convenience and ecological sustainability. AI Epistemic Disengagement and Consumer Dependence: An Augmentation‐Substitution Framework
2026-03-24
Vasilis Theoharakis, Nikolaos Mylonopoulos
AIartificial intelligence
EN

This study investigates how consumers justify accepting AI as a trusted reasoning source and when this acceptance preserves versus forfeits their capacity for independent thinking. Drawing on Bandura's moral disengagement theory, the authors introduce 'AI epistemic disengagement' and conduct two studies with nearly 600 US consumers to identify two distinct patterns of AI dependence: augmentation (maintaining epistemic responsibility through critical evaluation) and substitution (abdicating responsibility through uncritical acceptance). Key findings reveal that augmentation is associated with Need for Cognition, prompting skills, and more intense/habitual AI use, while substitution shows opposite patterns. The study contributes an augmentation-substitution framework that helps explain consumer AI dependence and offers practical implications for preserving consumer welfare during AI adoption.

2026-03-18

This research investigates how temporal cues in historical lowest price claims (distant vs. proximal) affect consumer purchase behavior. Time for Action: Implied Action Does It!
2026-03-16
Gudrun Roose, Maggie Geuens, Cristián Buzeta
AI
EN

This research investigates whether implied-action cues in static images—visual elements suggesting ongoing activity through objects in use without showing people or motion—can enhance consumer responses. Across four studies with 1,026 participants testing various product types (airlines, rentals, televisions), the authors found that implied-action cues positively influence brand attitudes, perceived quality, purchase intentions, and information-seeking behavior compared to no cues or non-action cues. Customer Experience: Heterogeneity Insights From Meta‐Analytic Synthesis
2026-03-12
Wagner Júnior Ladeira, Weng Marc Lim, Tareq Rasul, Fernando de Oliveira Santini, Raouf Ahmad Rather, et al.
AI
EN

This study addresses the overlooked issue of heterogeneity (variability) in customer experience (CX) research by conducting a meta-analytic review of 91 existing meta-analyses. Using Becker and Jaakkola's (2020) CX research classification framework, the authors identified moderators (conceptual, contextual, methodological, practical, and temporal) that explain variability across four research clusters: stimuli affecting experiences, responses to stimuli, evaluative outcomes, and customer contingencies. This research contributes to CX theory by systematically mapping sources of heterogeneity and provides practical guidance for managers and future researchers to account for contextual variations.

Transparency Matters: Psychological Ownership and Trust as Mediators of Explainable Artificial Intelligence Effectiveness
2026-03-10
Suresh Malodia, Yupal Shukla, Kirti Sharma, Anil Bilgihan
AIartificial intelligence
EN

This study examines how explainable AI (XAI) design influences consumers' intention to follow AI recommendations. Through four scenario-based experiments with 1,080 participants, the researchers investigated the effects of explanation level (high vs. low detail) and explanation type (process-oriented vs. outcome-oriented) across different recommendation contexts (product vs. content). The study contributes to the literature by identifying trust and psychological ownership as key mediating mechanisms through which XAI design translates into consumer action.

You Are Not Alone: Uptrend Messaging to Promote Green Consumption
2026-03-10
Yingyu Luo, Guowei Zhu, Jing Du, Jinfeng Lu, Jing Huang
AI
EN

This study investigates how uptrend messaging—communicating that green consumption is increasing—affects consumers' green product preferences. Through five studies including a field experiment, the authors demonstrate that uptrend messaging significantly enhances green consumption preferences by fostering response efficacy, the belief that purchasing green products will lead to desired environmental outcomes. The effect is strengthened when the uptrend magnitude is high or when consumers have an interdependent self-construal, but attenuated under low magnitude or independent self-construal conditions. These findings contribute to understanding how social trend information can serve as an effective behavioral intervention to narrow the attitude-behavior gap in sustainable consumption.

Fail to Win: How Schadenfreude and Competitive Traits Predict Download Intention in Mobile Game Fail Ads
2026-03-10
Moon Joonhyun, Yeolib Kim, Boreum Choi, Hanbyul Choi
AI
EN

This study investigates why mobile game advertisements depicting gameplay failures are effective, focusing on schadenfreude (pleasure from others' misfortune) and individual competitiveness as psychological mechanisms. Using two vignette-based experiments (N=241 and N=247), the authors found that hypercompetitiveness predicts schadenfreude, which in turn increases download intention, while perceived game difficulty moderates this effect—reasonably challenging failures evoke more schadenfreude than extremely difficult ones. The research contributes to understanding how negative emotional responses can drive positive consumer behavior and provides practical implications for marketers to optimize fail-based advertising by calibrating difficulty levels and targeting hypercompetitive consumers.

Built by Love: Romantic Relationship Motives and Do‐It‐Yourself Product Consumption
2026-03-07
Ali Gohary, Astrid Nørfelt, Eugene Y. Chan
AI
EN

This study investigates how romantic relationship motives influence consumers' preferences for do-it-yourself (DIY) products from an evolutionary psychology perspective. Through four main studies and two supplementary studies (N = 3,014), the researchers found that when relationship formation motives are activated, men prefer DIY products more than women due to greater risk-taking tendencies. However, women's DIY preferences increase when they engage in the process with a partner or experience heightened sense of power, and these effects are specific to utilitarian rather than hedonic contexts. The research contributes to understanding gendered consumption patterns and provides practical implications for marketers seeking to promote DIY products to both men and women.

Cognitive Overload and Emotional Arousal: Why Gambling Content Marketing Appeals to Children and Resists Established Educational Interventions
2026-03-07
Raffaello Rossi, Edoardo Tozzi, Agnes C. Nairn
AI
EN

This study investigates whether gambling content marketing on social media is more emotionally appealing and less recognizable as advertising to children compared to conventional gambling ads, and whether UK school-based educational interventions can mitigate these effects. Using an experiment with 58 children aged 13-17, the researchers measured appeal through combined self-reported emotional valence and physiological arousal (electrodermal activity), alongside advertising recognition via forced-choice tasks. Results showed that gambling content marketing was four times more appealing than conventional ads, with only 7% of children correctly identifying all content marketing as advertising, while the educational intervention had negligible effects. The study contributes to understanding why cognitive-based advertising literacy programs may be ineffective against emotionally engaging gambling content and demonstrates the value of combining self-report with physiological measures in assessing advertising appeal.

The Ironic Effects of Financial Constraint‐Induced Suppression on Consumer Behavior
2026-03-06
Jane So, Miao Miao, Yuki Nishida
AI
EN

This study examines how financial constraints lead consumers to suppress thoughts about their financial difficulties and spending desires as a coping mechanism, and whether this suppression triggers ironic rebound effects. Using three longitudinal studies, the authors found that financially constrained consumers engage in conscious thought suppression, which paradoxically causes suppressed thoughts to resurface more intensely when exposed to external cues like sales promotions, especially among consumers with high indulgence tendencies. The research contributes to the literature by identifying thought suppression as a cognitive mechanism underlying consumer responses to financial constraints and demonstrating the conditions under which rebound effects occur, offering practical implications for marketers.

New Customers or Infrequent Customers Over Frequent Customers? The Relative Spillover Effects of Acquisition Promotions and Activation Promotions
2026-03-06
Xinlan Li, Xingyu Wang, Yuhong Chang
EN

This study examines how frequent customers react differently to acquisition promotions (targeting new customers) versus activation promotions (targeting infrequent customers) when they are excluded from these targeted promotions. Through four online experiments, the authors find that frequent customers exhibit stronger negative reactions (lower retention intentions) when excluded from activation promotions compared to acquisition promotions, due to increased social comparison with targeted customers. The negative spillover effect is mitigated when customers discover the promotion online rather than offline, or when benefits are delayed rather than immediate. This research contributes to understanding the relative impacts of different targeted promotions on untargeted frequent customers and provides practical guidance for minimizing negative spillover effects.

Chatbots in Airlines: Are They Really Assisting?
2026-03-02
Pooja Kushwaha, Manoj Kumar Kamila
AIartificial intelligence
EN

This study investigates how value co-destruction processes in airline chatbot interactions contribute to customer dissatisfaction. Using a dual-method approach to analyze 6,800 records including user-generated reviews and texts, the research identifies four predominant themes: poor service quality, inadequate technology, lack of empathy, and unreliable information support. The findings inform the development of the Artificial Intelligence-Mediated Value Disconfirmation Model, which integrates multiple theoretical frameworks to explain how algorithmic opacity, emotional deficits, and interaction rigidity drive dissatisfaction. The study contributes theoretically by addressing AI-human complementarity paradoxes and practically by proposing three design principles—Emotion Alignment, Human Escalation, and Context Continuity—for developing emotion-aware chatbots.

What Makes People so Prone to Share Celebrity Gossip? A Combination of Less Guilt and More Excitement
2026-02-26
Gaia Giambastiani, Joseph C. Nunes, Andrea Ordanini
AI
EN

This research investigates why people are more inclined to gossip about their favorite celebrities compared to other targets, using a relational approach that contrasts parasocial relationships with balanced social relationships. Across five preregistered studies, the authors demonstrate that gossiping about one's favorite celebrity evokes less anticipated guilt and more perceived excitement than gossiping about friends, acquaintances, ex-partners, or non-favorite celebrities. The findings contribute to literature on word-of-mouth, parasocial relationships, and information sharing by revealing the psychological mechanisms underlying the prevalence of celebrity gossip.

2026-02-17
Sandra Pauser, Udo Wagner
AI
EN

This research investigates how salespeople's nonverbal expressiveness (facial expressions, body language, and vocal cues) affects customer responses during sales presentations, drawing on the Emotions as Social Information (EASI) model. Using innovative measurement techniques including facial recognition software, body action coding, and voice analysis, two large-scale experimental studies were conducted with videotaped sales presentations and real-time customer response tracking. EN

This study investigates how different athlete-brand relationship strategies (endorsements vs. co-investment and self-branding) affect consumer trust, drawing on signaling theory. The Rise of Human–Computer Integration in Marketing: A Theory Synthesis
2026-02-13
Carlos Velasco, Kosuke Motoki, Olivia Petit, Aram N. Andersen, Marzieh Alaei, et al.
AI
EN

This article addresses how human–computer integration (HCInt) technologies—ranging from wearables to brain–computer implants—transform consumer experience by merging bodily, cognitive, and sensory functions with computational processes. Using a theory synthesis approach that integrates insights from human–computer interaction, psychology, and marketing, the authors develop a marketing-oriented conceptual model distinguishing between fusion (technology as bodily extension) and symbiosis (adaptive human–AI co-regulation). The key finding is that these integration modes operate through neurocognitive mechanisms of embodiment, peripersonal space, and bodily self-consciousness to generate experiential coupling throughout the customer journey. The paper contributes by extending embodiment and customer-experience frameworks to the HCInt domain while identifying opportunities (inclusion, personalization) and challenges (privacy, inequality, mental integrity threats) for future empirical and ethical research.

How to Conduct a Multi‐Domain Systematic (Literature) Review? Guidelines Using The Lotus Protocol
2026-02-10
Bart J. A. van Bueren, Weng Marc Lim, Naveen Donthu, Kevin Argus, Julian M. Water‐Lynch, et al.
AI
EN

This article addresses the challenge of conducting systematic literature reviews across multiple intersecting knowledge domains, as existing protocols typically limit researchers to single-domain or single-intersection reviews. The authors introduce The Lotus Protocol, a systematic multi-domain approach visualized through Venn diagram-like petal structures, consisting of three phases: conceptual exploration, corpora creation, and contribution capture. Demonstrated through a review synthesizing food waste, behavioral change, mobile applications, and revenue domains, the protocol identified four value propositions for food-waste-fighting apps (brokering, delivery, inventory planning, and education) while revealing theoretical gaps regarding planetary boundaries and behavioral science. The Lotus Protocol contributes a formalized methodology for synthesizing evidence across multiple knowledge domains, positioning systematic reviews as translational research capable of generating holistic insights for addressing complex, multidisciplinary grand challenges.

Experience Therapy: How Service Experiences Regulate Everyday Negative Affect
2026-02-07
Chang Ma, Jae Eun Park, Alei Fan, Laurie Wu
AI
EN

This research examines how service experiences can serve as 'experience therapy' to regulate everyday negative affect in high-pressure modern society. Through a series of studies, the authors investigate how different service experiences—varying in personal effort involvement and social involvement—contribute to reducing negative emotions. The findings reveal that experiences requiring high personal effort and high social involvement are particularly effective in alleviating negative affect among emotionally stressed individuals, with these effects mediated by increased self-efficacy and social support respectively. This work advances theoretical understanding of affect regulation through service experiences and provides practical implications for designing emotionally restorative service encounters.

JA

本研究は、現代の高ストレス社会において、サービス体験が日常的なネガティブ感情の調整にどのように寄与するかを検証し、「体験セラピー」という新たな感情調整戦略を提唱している。一連の研究を通じて、個人的努力の関与度と社会的関与度が異なるサービス体験がネガティブ感情の軽減にどう影響するかを調査した。その結果、高い個人的努力と高い社会的関与を必要とする体験が、特に感情的ストレスを抱える人々のネガティブ感情緩和に効果的であり、これらの効果はそれぞれ自己効力感の向上と社会的サポートによって媒介されることが明らかになった。本研究はサービス体験と感情調整に関する理論的理解を深め、感情回復を促すサービス設計への実践的示唆を提供している。

An Integrative Framework and Research Agenda for Diversity, Equity, and Inclusion in AI‐Driven Marketing
2026-02-06
Darius-Aurel; id_orcid 0000-0002-1577-7352 Frank, Raphaela Bruckdorfer, Lina Jacobsen, Yi Li, Tobias Otterbring
AIartificial intelligence
EN

This article examines whether AI-driven marketing advances or hinders diversity, equity, and inclusion (DEI) in the marketplace. Through a synthesis of existing research, the authors develop an integrative framework organized around three transformational themes: from design to practice, from implementation to integration, and from evaluation to empowerment. The study identifies that while AI offers potential benefits such as expanded services to wider customer bases, it also poses risks including bias replication and threats to human autonomy. The contribution lies in providing a comprehensive research agenda that addresses grand challenges and trends, offering guidance for researchers, marketers, and policymakers seeking to leverage AI while promoting DEI.

JA

本論文は、AIを活用したマーケティングが市場における多様性・公平性・包摂性（DEI）を促進するのか、それとも阻害するのかという問いを検討している。既存研究の統合を通じて、「設計から実践へ」「実装から統合へ」「評価からエンパワーメントへ」という3つの変革的テーマを軸とした統合的フレームワークを提案している。研究の結果、AIはより幅広い顧客層へのサービス拡大という可能性を持つ一方で、バイアスの再生産や人間の自律性への脅威といったリスクも存在することが明らかにされた。本論文の貢献は、DEIに関する大きな課題とトレンドに対応する包括的な研究アジェンダを提示し、AIの力を活用しながらDEIを推進しようとする研究者・マーケター・政策立案者に指針を提供した点にある。

Digital Cognition in Predictive Marketing Personalization: A Conceptual Framework
2026-02-06
José Ramón Saura, Vanessa Ratten, Veljko Jeremić
AIartificial intelligence
EN

This study addresses the lack of an integrative theoretical framework explaining how predictive personalization recursively interacts with consumer cognition in digital marketing. Using systematic literature review and qualitative synthesis, the authors develop a conceptual framework that models digital cognition as a closed-loop system where behavioral data feeds AI-driven algorithms that reshape users' attention, memory, and decision processes. The key finding reveals that predictive marketing personalization creates a self-modifying system in which human cognition and machine logic co-evolve, introducing the concept of 'cognitive equilibrium marketing' to balance algorithmic precision with ethical considerations. The study contributes 7 research propositions and 30 future research questions, offering both theoretical integration across fragmented literatures and practical guidance for designing cognition-aware personalization systems.

JA

本研究は、予測的パーソナライゼーションが消費者の認知とどのように再帰的に相互作用するかを説明する統合的理論フレームワークの欠如という課題に取り組んでいる。体系的文献レビューと質的統合を用いて、行動データがAI駆動アルゴリズムに供給され、ユーザーの注意・記憶・意思決定プロセスを再形成する閉ループシステムとしてデジタル認知をモデル化する概念的フレームワークを構築した。主要な発見として、予測的マーケティング・パーソナライゼーションが人間の認知と機械のロジックが共進化する自己修正システムを生み出すことを明らかにし、アルゴリズムの精度と倫理的配慮のバランスを取る「認知均衡マーケティング」の概念を導入した。本研究は7つの研究命題と30の将来研究課題を提示し、断片化した文献の理論的統合と認知対応型パーソナライゼーションシステム設計への実践的指針の両方に貢献している。

Sounds Sweet: Sound Reduplication in Brand Names Enhances Sweet Taste Expectations
2026-01-31
Kosuke Motoki, Sayo Iseki, Abhishek Pathak
AI
EN

This study investigates how sound reduplication in brand names affects consumers' sweet taste expectations. Across seven studies, the researchers found that brands with reduplicated sounds (e.g., repeated syllables) consistently evoke stronger expectations of sweetness, an effect mediated by perceptions of baby schema cuteness. The findings also revealed that this effect is moderated by consumers' sweet-liker status and brand positioning (mass-market vs. luxury). This research contributes to marketing literature by identifying sound reduplication as a novel phonetic cue that can be strategically used in food branding to signal sweetness.

JA

本研究は、ブランド名における音の重複（畳語）が消費者の甘味期待にどのような影響を与えるかを検討した。7つの研究を通じて、音の重複を含むブランド名は一貫して甘味の期待を高めることが示され、この効果はベビースキーマに基づく「かわいさ」の知覚によって媒介されることが明らかになった。さらに、甘味嗜好者かどうか、およびブランドのポジショニング（マス市場向けか高級ブランドか）がこの効果を調整することも判明した。本研究は、音の重複が食品ブランディングにおいて甘味を示唆する新たな音声的手がかりとなりうることを示し、マーケティング研究に貢献している。

Dynamic Evolution and Transformative Trends in the Consumer Market: A Technology Paradox Perspective
2026-01-31
Chanaka Jayawardhena, Hongfei Liu, Chatura Ranaweera, P.K. Senyo
AI
EN

This paper addresses the lack of a unifying framework to explain contradictions arising from technological advancement and consumer psychology in the consumer market. The authors introduce a conceptual model based on technology paradox theory that maps the dynamic process from antecedents (technological and psycho-cultural forces) to consumer outcomes through core tensions and coping strategies. By synthesizing eight transformative trends through this lens, the study reveals underlying psychological mechanisms and proposes a model-driven research agenda for future scholarship. The paper contributes both theoretically by providing an integrative framework and practically by offering managers three specific strategies—ethical AI design with transparent data practices, authentic inclusivity, and sustainable innovation—to navigate market paradoxes.

JA

本論文は、消費者市場における技術進歩と消費者心理の衝突から生じる矛盾を説明する統一的フレームワークの欠如という課題に取り組んでいる。著者らはテクノロジー・パラドックス理論に基づく概念モデルを提示し、先行要因（技術的・心理文化的要因）から核心的緊張関係（例：パーソナライゼーション対プライバシー）と対処戦略を経て消費者アウトカムに至る動的プロセスをマッピングした。このレンズを通じて8つの変革的トレンドを統合し、その背後にある心理的メカニズムを明らかにするとともに、将来の研究に向けたモデル駆動型の研究アジェンダを提案している。理論的には統合的フレームワークの提供、実務的には倫理的AI設計と透明なデータ慣行、真正なインクルーシビティ、持続可能なイノベーションという3つの戦略を通じて、管理者が市場のパラドックスを乗り越えるための指針を示した点で貢献している。
